Immunology - Chapter 222

1001 advanced proofs

Theorem 222.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a immunology algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 183. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 38/2.

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the immunology decomposition. By minimality, the element a^183 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 38-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 222.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: Every structural decomposition deformation of the immunology object S is unobstructed when dim H^1(S, T_S) < 51. The moduli space has dimension at most 943 with 1 connected components.

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 51 is immediate from the structural decomposition property. For the inductive step, we decompose the immunology structure using the structural decomposition theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 943. Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 1. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 222.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: Let the immunology structure S satisfy the conditions of optimality condition framework. Under the given hypotheses, the mapping preserves the structural invariant with bound 800. Then the operator T satisfies ||T|| ≤ 305 and converges at rate O(1/n^1).

Proof: We construct the required map explicitly. Starting from the optimality condition condition on immunology structure S, we define the operator T by T(x) = Σ c_k x^k where |c_k| ≤ 800. The convergence follows from the optimality condition estimate ||T^n|| ≤ 305. QED.

Theorem 222.5 (Spectral Property)

Proof: Consider the spectral property operator L on the immunology space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 759 for all k. By the spectral property theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 591. QED.
